=== port-1 wedge hypothesis ===
first tunable entry targeting an INACTIVE port slice:
  apcie-phy-ip-auspma-tunables entry #30: offset=0x12000 -> target_addr=0x497052000
If m1n1's tunable applicator hits this address, the AXI
write to an unpowered slave hangs the fabric silently.
This matches the observed wedge in pcie_up_2.log.

=== extra apcie tunables report (t8132-specific, from ADT, no MMIO) ===
These are the two apcie-node tunable properties that
m1n1's pcie.c does NOT apply on the T8140 codepath.
They may be the missing PCIe clock/PLL setup that the
phy_ip window needs before its MMIO becomes reachable.

=== applied apcie tunables report (from ADT, no MMIO) ===
These are the tunable properties m1n1 pcie.c DOES apply
on the T8140 codepath. Listed so we can eyeball for
any hidden clock-enable / PLL-config write in the
phy_shared window that could be gating phy_ip.

=== naked-write test READ-ONLY (RUN 4: first-touch only, no writes) (RUN Q base + RUN R extensions) ===
purpose: determine whether writes to rc_base/axi_base
actually stick when we bypass m1n1 tunables_apply_local.
RUN O's reachable-scan showed the applicator's writes
to these blocks silently no-op'd; naked writes here
answer whether the applicator is broken (writes will
stick) or the fabric drops all writes (they won't).
RUN R adds first-touch probes for axi_sub5/sub6
(candidate CIO3 PLL Core target blocks) plus
additional rc_base offsets (0x54 R/W bitmap, 0x100
cio3pllcore #6 candidate).
RUN 4: write stage suppressed. Pre-read still flips
axi_sub{5,6}_reachable on success so downstream
reachable-scan + naked-extra applies can proceed
without destroying iBoot's sub5+0 PLL control word.
